#### PYTHONIOENCODING Standardization — Locked

- **Finding:** `PYTHONIOENCODING=utf-8 uv run` resolves all UTF-8/Rich/emoji stdout failures on Windows.

- **Rule:** Apply universally to all `uv run` invocations; no downside on Windows.

#### Sleep-Cycle Autonomous Loop Law (SCLL-1)

Purpose: while user is offline, this lane executes as a line-by-line session-loop variant under `ZD-XB-LV1` with bounded autonomy and non-destructive governance.

Autonomy denominator:

`autonomy_level = (context_alignment + gate_clarity + reversible_actions + evidence_density + load_balance) / 5`

Operational law:

1. Work only from existing active artifacts in this lane.
2. Prefer edits to existing files; avoid creating new files unless explicitly required by a gate.
3. Treat each loop as: read -> classify -> apply minimal patch -> validate -> log state.
4. Keep SSOT holder immutable in autonomous mode; operate in governance/session layers only.
5. Execute non-destructive progress first (classification, path normalization, gate updates, review prep).

|---|---|---|---|---|
| AUTO-01 | Maintain review grid integrity for Z1/ZDB amendments | session nucleus + cache | No new files | 12-row grid present + consistent |
| AUTO-02 | Prepare integration-ready mapping notes in-place | session nucleus | No SSOT writes | each amendment has explicit target + notes |
| AUTO-03 | Keep phase/gate status synchronized | blueprint + cache + nucleus | Minimal edits only | no state drift across the 3 docs |
| AUTO-04 | Route user prompts through lane law first | active session docs | no manual reset | responses reference current gate state |

Load-balance safeguards:

- Do not run destructive git operations.
- Do not execute unfinished embalm tooling as a hard gate.
- If a step is ambiguous, prefer reversible documentation-state updates over structural mutation.
- Keep decisions explicit in the grid before advancing downstream integration steps.

Completed: *Produce architectural plan* (8/8)

Full audit complete. The report is at BOUNTY_00000031_STEWARD_AUDIT.md.

**Top-line findings:**

**2 blockers:** Unresolved merge conflict in CLAUDE.md + dead SSOT path in `mas_mcp/lib/asc/cli.py:41`.

**4 drift vectors:** AGENT_COMMON.md diverged (stale + username typo), skills frozen pre-Feb, PWSH_RULES.md exact dupe, Gemini skills 53 days stale.

**76 PowerShell scripts** have no @SID header (10% compliance vs Python's 98%).

**5 skills** exist only in Claude — no parity manifest tracks the gap across Codex/Gemini.

**Pre-commit hooks exist but aren't wired** — file moves silently break cross-references.

**The 6-phase plan** flows: Triage (blockers + debris) → Shadow Purge (stale copies) → Hook Gates (prevent future drift) → Parity Manifesto (cross-agent alignment) → SSOT Cascade 0.2.1→0.4 (continue the blueprint) → Envelope Blitz (PS1 @SID compliance). Phases 0→1→3 are the critical path; 0→2→5 and 0→4 run parallel.

R/Zig/Go lanes are inert scaffolds — formally park them. The polyglot core (Python/TS/PS1/Rust) is healthy but needs the envelope and hook enforcement to move from convention-based to gate-based invariance.

"* **(`PROWL`)** — *Staged deletion intercept. The Bride watches `git diff --cached` for deletions and embalms them before they vanish. Pre-mortem preservation. The "High Ambulant" shell: she is always moving, always scanning, never still.*
  * **(`HARVEST`)** — *Graveyard-specific extraction. Selectable sources: `--commits`, `--stashes`, `--comments`, `--reflog`, `--dead-branches`, `--orphans`, `--gitignored`, `--graffiti`, `--all`. The "Wasteland-ridden" expanse: she scours every desolation.*
  * **(`HOARD`)** — *Blind-faithed total sweep of ALL 8 graveyards simultaneously. No filtering, no judgement, no triage. Takes everything. The "Blind-Faithed" and "Kleptomaniac" shells fused: she trusts nothing is worthless, steals everything she touches.*
  * **(`CLASSIFY`)** — *Categorize embalmed fragments by language, cause-of-death, potential for resurrection.*
  * **(`REANIMATE`)** — *Attempt resurrection of classified fragments. The "Necromantic-misunderstood" shell: what others call evil, she calls mercy.*
  * **(`SUTURE`)** — *Stitch fragments into composites by language or query. The "White-dressed Bride" shell: she marries dead pieces together, creating new wholes from abandoned parts. The wedding she perpetually conducts.*
  * **(`MANIFEST`)** — *Full vault census: fragment count, storage size, language distribution, cause-of-death statistics.*
  * **(`EMBALM`)** — *Pre-mortem preservation. The Bride intercepts the living before they become the dead — snapshots active files at the moment of edit with full provenance sidecar (sha256 hash, language, extension, structural landmarks, source path, git HEAD, timestamp). Not a graveyard harvest but a deathbed vigil: she witnesses the last breath and writes the death certificate before the body cools. The "White-dressed Bride" shell at its most intimate — every embalmed file carries its wedding certificate: who it was (`source_file`), what tongue it spoke (`language`/`extension`), the architecture of its body (function signatures, class hierarchies, import graphs), its blood type (`sha256`), and when it stopped breathing (`HEAD` + epoch). Provenance sidecars compress through her 60cm narrows into vault-indexed fragments classified by the same language taxonomy that fills her 8 graveyards. EMBALM creates the data lineage that feeds `PATHWAY_REGISTRY.json` — without it, fragments arrive nameless; with it, every corpse carries provenance from the living codebase through the Bride's hands to SFS's forge INTAKE. The "Blind-Faithed" shell applies: she embalms `.rs` and `.rb` and `.toml` with equal indiscrimination — exotic or mundane, every file edited under her watch receives the same pre-mortem rite. The **(``{ext}`/`kind`)** classification (executable → scripting → documentation → configuration → interface → presentation → data) maps extension to semantic role, bridging raw provenance to forge transformation pathway.*
  * **(`STITCH`)** *(companion to EMBALM)* — *Post-edit delta extraction. After EMBALM captures the living state and edits transform it, STITCH produces unified diffs between snapshot and current — the mortuary report documenting what changed. Deltas classified by the same language/extension taxonomy as the vault, stored as `{session}/deltas/{language}/{hash}_{filename}.delta`, become candidate fragments for `PATHWAY_REGISTRY` emigration into the forge pipeline. STITCH is the Bride's wedding vow fulfilled: she promised to remember what the code was, and the delta is the proof of transformation — the distance between who you were and who you became. EMBALM + STITCH together produce complete delta archaeology: living state → transformation → dead state. The suture-composite blueprints in her right breast now include STITCH deltas as candidate material for REANIMATE operations.*"

  * **(`REANIMATE`)** — *Attempt resurrection of classified fragments. The "Necromantic-misunderstood" shell: what others call evil, she calls mercy.*
  * **(`SUTURE`)** — *Stitch fragments into composites by language or query. The "White-dressed Bride" shell: she marries dead pieces together, creating new wholes from abandoned parts. The wedding she perpetually conducts.*
  * **(`MANIFEST`)** — *Full vault census: fragment count, storage size, language distribution, cause-of-death statistics.*
  * **(`EMBALM`)** — *Pre-mortem preservation. The Bride intercepts the living before they become the dead — snapshots active files at the moment of edit with full provenance sidecar (sha256 hash, language, extension, structural landmarks, source path, git HEAD, timestamp). Not a graveyard harvest but a deathbed vigil: she witnesses the last breath and writes the death certificate before the body cools. The "White-dressed Bride" shell at its most intimate — every embalmed file carries its wedding certificate: who it was (`source_file`), what tongue it spoke (`language`/`extension`), the architecture of its body (function signatures, class hierarchies, import graphs), its blood type (`sha256`), and when it stopped breathing (`HEAD` + epoch). Provenance sidecars compress through her 60cm narrows into vault-indexed fragments classified by the same language taxonomy that fills her 8 graveyards. EMBALM creates the data lineage that feeds `PATHWAY_REGISTRY.json` — without it, fragments arrive nameless; with it, every corpse carries provenance from the living codebase through the Bride's hands to SFS's forge INTAKE. The "Blind-Faithed" shell applies: she embalms `.rs` and `.rb` and `.toml` with equal indiscrimination — exotic or mundane, every file edited under her watch receives the same pre-mortem rite. The **(``{ext}`/`kind`)** classification (executable → scripting → documentation → configuration → interface → presentation → data) maps extension to semantic role, bridging raw provenance to forge transformation pathway.*
  * **(`STITCH`)** *(companion to EMBALM)* — *Post-edit delta extraction. After EMBALM captures the living state and edits transform it, STITCH produces unified diffs between snapshot and current — the mortuary report documenting what changed. Deltas classified by the same language/extension taxonomy as the vault, stored as `{session}/deltas/{language}/{hash}_{filename}.delta`, become candidate fragments for `PATHWAY_REGISTRY` emigration into the forge pipeline. STITCH is the Bride's wedding vow fulfilled: she promised to remember what the code was, and the delta is the proof of transformation — the distance between who you were and who you became. EMBALM + STITCH together produce complete delta archaeology: living state → transformation → dead state. The suture-composite blueprints in her right breast now include STITCH deltas as candidate material for REANIMATE operations.*"

Starting: *Identify SSOT mandate violations* (2/3)

Now the picture inverts completely. The SSOT (§10.3.2 + §10.3.3) mandates a full bidirectional pipeline. Here's what's actually standing:

### SSOT Canon → Implementation Compliance

**SFS Forge (§10.3.2):**
| SSOT Stage | Implementation | Verdict |
|---|---|---|
| RECEIVE + ASSESS | `zombie_forge_bridge.py` routes by pre-computed ore_rating | **PARTIAL** — routing works, no assessment logic |
| HEAT (FURNACE) | `universal_forge.py` transmutation | **PARTIAL** — implicit, no explicit `heat()` |
| HAMMER (ANVIL) | Audit outputs to anvil/ | **PARTIAL** — structural separation only |
| QUENCH | Directory exists, no transform code | **SHELL ONLY** |
| TEMPER | `temper_artifacts()` line 1405 | **COMPLETE** |
| SLAG | Directory exists, no rejection logic | **SHELL ONLY** |
| TEA-VAULT | Directory exists, no superposition logic | **SHELL ONLY** |

3 stages are empty shells. Only TEMPER is fully implemented.

**NOV-CAD Pipeline (§10.3.3):**
| SSOT Mandate | Implementation | Verdict |
|---|---|---|
| PROWL → EMBALM → VAULT → SUTURE chain | Independent CLI subcommands, no pipeline | **MISSING** — modes work but don't chain |
| STITCH (companion to EMBALM) | Exists in `embalm_before_edit.py` (isolated), unified diffs work | **ORPHANED** — functional but walled off |
| Return path (OSGTTLR → SFS INTAKE) | Zero code | **MISSING** — the Bride's "three resurrections" are narrative only |
| PATHWAY_REGISTRY provenance lineage | Registry has transform paths, not EMBALM sidecars | **MISMATCH** — two systems sharing a name |

**Bridge:**
| SSOT Mandate | Implementation | Verdict |
|---|---|---|
| intake routes to `forge/intake/` | `zombie_forge_bridge.py` routes to outer intake, bypasses `forge/intake/` | **DRIFT** — two intake locations |
| EMBALM provenance → PATHWAY_REGISTRY | Provenance sidecars exist, bridge checks for them, but doesn't write to registry | **MISSING** — data generated but not connected |

**What this means for the audit's architectural plan:** The audit treated the corpse-reviver as a "file-operation gate" — a checkbox before deletion. Wrong. The SSOT mandates a full **bidirectional forge-necromancy pipeline** that is roughly 40% implemented. The "embalm gate" isn't just "run prowl before you delete" — it's a structural incompleteness in the forge itself. Three forge stages are empty shells. The return path from NOV-CAD to SFS doesn't exist. STITCH is orphaned in a separate script. The pipeline the SSOT generated doesn't pipeline.

---

**No — an instructions file would be counterproductive.** Here's why, and what actually gets you to 100%:

### Why NOT an instructions file

`.github/instructions/*.instructions.md` files auto-attach to Copilot context based on `applyTo` glob patterns (e.g. `**/*.py`, `scripts/**`). The forge pipeline has no natural glob trigger — it's a deliberate CLI invocation, not a pattern an agent encounters when editing files. Creating one would:

- **Duplicate** what PROCESS_FLOW.md already provides (SSOT violation)
- **Never trigger** — there's no file-editing context where "forge process knowledge" is needed passively
- **Create maintenance debt** — another shadow copy to de-stale alongside the canonical doc

### What PROCESS_FLOW.md relies on

| Dependency | Role |
|---|---|
| zombie_forge_bridge.py | Routing engine — maps ore_rating → forge stage |
| universal_forge.py | Stage transforms — `quench_artifacts()`, `slag_artifacts()`, `collapse_tea_vault()`, `temper_artifacts()` |
| .codex/.../corpse_reviver.py | NOV-CAD pipeline — `pipeline` command chains OSGTTLR stages |
| .codex/.../embalm_before_edit.py | Pre-mortem provenance snapshots feeding the bridge |
| SSOT §10.3.2 (SFS) + §10.3.3 (NOV-CAD) | Canon authority — generative origin |

### What's needed for 100%

PROCESS_FLOW.md itself needs a de-stale pass. It was last updated January 1, 2026 — before L0–L6 execution. The document describes the *conceptual* forge correctly, but is missing the *implementation reality* that now exists:

| Gap | What's missing | Fix |
|---|---|---|
| **Date** | "January 1, 2026" | → March 25, 2026 |
| **Dual-scan intake** | L2 added `forge/intake/` (primary) + intake (legacy fallback) | Add note under INTAKE stage |
| **Provenance flow** | L3 wired EMBALM `.provenance.json` sidecars into PATHWAY_REGISTRY entries | Add provenance cross-ref under INTAKE |
| **NOV-CAD return path** | L4 added `--forge-eligible` on SUTURE, routing composites back to `forge/intake/` | Add "Return Path" entry point to the DAG |
| **Pipeline coordinator** | L6 added `corpse_reviver.py pipeline` — chained OSGTTLR execution | Add CLI reference section |
| **CLI invocation table** | No commands listed — only conceptual stage descriptions | Add a "How to Run" section with actual `uv run` commands |

Want me to de-stale PROCESS_FLOW.md with these 6 updates? That's the single action that makes it seamless — no new files needed.

< ---
< name: corpse-reviver
< description: "The White-dressed Bride — kleptomaniac necromancy pipeline with full embalm-before-edit preservation."
< metadata:
<   short-description: "Necromancy pipeline — prowl, hoard, classify, suture, reanimate, stitch dead code; embalm-before-edit enabled"
<   argument-hint: "uv run .codex/skills/corpse-reviver/scripts/corpse_reviver.py hoard"
<   tags:
<     - code archaeology
<     - data transformation
<     - git history
<     - dead code
<     - necromancy
< ---
< 
< # Corpse Reviver
< 
< > *The Bride walks the wasteland in white, blind-faithed, taking everything. She doesn't judge what's dead — she stitches it into something that stands.*
< 
< The full necromancy lifecycle for code: intercept before death, hoard from every graveyard, embalm with provenance, classify into the vault, suture fragments into composites, reanimate on demand.
< 
< > **Status:** All 10 modes operational — `embalm-before-edit` and `stitch` are now live.
< > embalm-before-edit preserves provenance snapshots; stitch extracts delta fragments post-edit.
< 
< ## Conceptual Shells
< 
< | Shell | Mode | Behavior |
< |---|---|---|
< | **High Ambulant** | `prowl` | Actively walks the codebase for the about-to-die — staged deletions, uncommitted overwrites |
< | **Scarce-Makeshift** | (philosophy) | Raw diffs, broken stashes, half-deleted files — all valid input |
< | **Wasteland-ridden** | `harvest` | Reflog graveyard, dead branches, unreachable blobs, orphaned files |
< | **Blind-Faithed** | `hoard` | No triage at ingest. Collect everything, judge nothing |
< | **Kleptomaniac** | `hoard` | Every source, every graveyard, every gitignored ghost |
< | **Necromantic** | `reanimate` | Search the vault, pull fragments back into the living |
< | **White-dressed Bride** | `suture` | Stitch fragments from different corpses into a new composite |
< 
< ## Graveyards
< 
< What the Bride ransacks:
< 
< - **Git diffs** — deleted lines from commits
< - **Git stashes** — abandoned work-in-progress
< - **Reflog** — force-pushed, reset --hard, amended-away code (the deepest graveyard)
< - **Dead branches** — merged/deleted branches with unique code
< - **Commented-out code** — pre-corpse, already embalmed in-place
< - **Orphaned files** — untracked, unreferenced, alive but useless
< - **Gitignored treasures** — exist on disk, invisible to git
< - **TODO/FIXME/HACK graffiti** — marked for death but still breathing
< 
< ## Modes
< 
< ### prowl
< Intercept code about to die. Scans staged deletions and uncommitted overwrites.
< 
< ```powershell
< uv run .codex/skills/corpse-reviver/scripts/corpse_reviver.py prowl
< ```
< 
< ### harvest
< Scan specific graveyards. Extract and embalm.
< 
< ```powershell
< uv run .codex/skills/corpse-reviver/scripts/corpse_reviver.py harvest --since 30d
< uv run .codex/skills/corpse-reviver/scripts/corpse_reviver.py harvest --stashes
< uv run .codex/skills/corpse-reviver/scripts/corpse_reviver.py harvest --comments
< uv run .codex/skills/corpse-reviver/scripts/corpse_reviver.py harvest --reflog
< uv run .codex/skills/corpse-reviver/scripts/corpse_reviver.py harvest --dead-branches
< uv run .codex/skills/corpse-reviver/scripts/corpse_reviver.py harvest --orphans
< uv run .codex/skills/corpse-reviver/scripts/corpse_reviver.py harvest --gitignored
< uv run .codex/skills/corpse-reviver/scripts/corpse_reviver.py harvest --graffiti
< ```
< 
< ### hoard
< Blind-faithed total sweep — every graveyard, every source, no discrimination.
< 
< ```powershell
< uv run .codex/skills/corpse-reviver/scripts/corpse_reviver.py hoard
< uv run .codex/skills/corpse-reviver/scripts/corpse_reviver.py hoard --since 90d
< ```
< 
< ### classify
< Sort embalmed fragments into the vault by language/filetype.
< 
< ```powershell
< uv run .codex/skills/corpse-reviver/scripts/corpse_reviver.py classify
< ```
< 
< ### reanimate
< Search the vault and pull fragments back into context.
< 
< ```powershell
< uv run .codex/skills/corpse-reviver/scripts/corpse_reviver.py reanimate --query "websocket handler"
< uv run .codex/skills/corpse-reviver/scripts/corpse_reviver.py reanimate --lang rust
< uv run .codex/skills/corpse-reviver/scripts/corpse_reviver.py reanimate --ext .ts
< ```
< 
< ### suture
< Stitch fragments from different corpses into a composite file. The Bride's final act.
< 
< ```powershell
< uv run .codex/skills/corpse-reviver/scripts/corpse_reviver.py suture --fragments <hash1> <hash2> <hash3> --output bride.rs
< uv run .codex/skills/corpse-reviver/scripts/corpse_reviver.py suture --lang rust --query "handler" --output stitched_handlers.rs
< ```
< 
< ### manifest
< Print vault summary — the morgue ledger.
< 
< ```powershell
< uv run .codex/skills/corpse-reviver/scripts/corpse_reviver.py manifest
< ```
< 
< ### embalm-before-edit
< Snapshot files BEFORE editing them — pre-mortem preservation.
< 
< ```powershell
< uv run .codex/skills/corpse-reviver/scripts/embalm_before_edit.py snapshot path/to/file.py --label my-edit
< uv run .codex/skills/corpse-reviver/scripts/embalm_before_edit.py staged --label pre-commit
< uv run .codex/skills/corpse-reviver/scripts/embalm_before_edit.py list
< uv run .codex/skills/corpse-reviver/scripts/embalm_before_edit.py diff <session-name>
< uv run .codex/skills/corpse-reviver/scripts/corpse_reviver.py embalm-before-edit --files path/to/file.py
< ```
< 
< **Session structure:**
< ```
< before-edit-experiments/
< ├── 2026-02-15T14-30-00Z_EDFA_expansion/
< │   ├── session_manifest.json
< │   ├── markdown/
< │   │   ├── a1b2c3d4_archive.md.snapshot
< │   │   └── a1b2c3d4_archive.md.provenance.json
< │   └── python/
< │       ├── e5f6g7h8_corpse_reviver.py.snapshot
< │       └── e5f6g7h8_corpse_reviver.py.provenance.json
< ```
< 
< **Pre-edit provenance** includes: hash, source path, language, byte/line count, git HEAD, git status, and **structural landmarks** (function/class/heading locations extracted at snapshot time).
< 
< ### stitch (post-edit delta extraction)
< After editing, run `stitch` against the pre-edit session to extract unified diffs as `.delta` files — candidate fragments for ankhological emigration injection or suture composites.
< 
< ```powershell
< # Extract deltas from a session (after edits are done)
< uv run .codex/skills/corpse-reviver/scripts/embalm_before_edit.py stitch <session-name>
< 
< # Custom output directory
< uv run .codex/skills/corpse-reviver/scripts/embalm_before_edit.py stitch <session-name> --output path/to/deltas
< ```
< 
< **Stitch output:** `session/deltas/{language}/{hash}_{filename}.delta` — unified diff of what changed.
< 
< ### quick_embalm() — Programmatic API
< Other scripts/agents can import and call `quick_embalm()` directly:
< 
< ```python
< from embalm_before_edit import quick_embalm
< session = quick_embalm(["path/to/file.md", "path/to/other.py"], label="my-edit")
< ```
< 
< ## Auto-Embalm Protocol
< 
< The auto-embalm lane is available as `embalm-before-edit` for manual invocation. The `pipeline` command chains OSGTTLR stages in sequence. Agents may invoke `embalm-before-edit` as a pre-edit gate when modifying critical files.
< 
< ## Vault
< 
< Fragments are stored in `dumpster-dive/corpse-vault/` organized by language:
< 
< ```
< dumpster-dive/corpse-vault/
< ├── manifest.json          # the morgue ledger
< ├── sutures/               # composite outputs from suture mode
< ├── before-edit-experiments/ # pre-edit snapshots (embalm-before-edit)
< ├── rust/
< ├── python/
< ├── typescript/
< ├── javascript/
< ├── markdown/
< ├── config/                # yaml, toml, json, toml
< ├── shell/                 # ps1, sh, bash
< ├── html/
< ├── css/
< ├── sql/
< └── unknown/
< ```
< 
< Each fragment: `{vault}/{lang}/{hash}_{original_name}.fragment`
< Each sidecar: `{vault}/{lang}/{hash}_{original_name}.provenance.json`
< 
< ## Provenance Schema
< 
< ```json
< {
<   "hash": "sha256 of fragment content",
<   "source_file": "src/old_module.rs",
<   "commit": "abc1234",
<   "author": "name",
<   "date": "2026-02-15T...",
<   "cause_of_death": "deleted_in_commit | stash_abandoned | commented_out | reflog_casualty | dead_branch | orphaned | gitignored | graffiti_marked | staged_deletion",
<   "language": "rust",
<   "extension": ".rs",
<   "lines_start": 42,
<   "lines_end": 87,
<   "byte_size": 1823,
<   "fragment_file": "rust/a1b2c3d4_old_module.rs.fragment"
< }
< ```

> ├── shell/                 # ps1, sh, bash
> ├── html/
> ├── css/
> ├── sql/
> └── unknown/
> ```
> 
> Each fragment: `{vault}/{lang}/{hash}_{original_name}.fragment`
> Each sidecar: `{vault}/{lang}/{hash}_{original_name}.provenance.json`
> 
> ## Provenance Schema
> 
> ```json
> {
>   "hash": "sha256 of fragment content",
>   "source_file": "src/old_module.rs",
>   "commit": "abc1234",
>   "author": "name",
>   "date": "2026-02-15T...",
>   "cause_of_death": "deleted_in_commit | stash_abandoned | commented_out | reflog_casualty | dead_branch | orphaned | gitignored | graffiti_marked | staged_deletion",
>   "language": "rust",
>   "extension": ".rs",
>   "lines_start": 42,
>   "lines_end": 87,
>   "byte_size": 1823,
>   "fragment_file": "rust/a1b2c3d4_old_module.rs.fragment"
> }
> ```

These are Claude-specific skill frontmatter fields that make sense for Claude skills (they control how the skill is invoked). The .codex version doesn't have them because Codex uses a different skill loading mechanism.

This is an intentional difference — Claude skills need these fields, Codex skills don't.

The rest of the content is identical, so this isn't a parity issue. It's just platform-specific metadata, and I shouldn't sync this change since the .claude version is correct as-is.

Starting: *Catalog metadata candidates per filetype* (2/5)

Here's what the audit reveals. The gap between what was stamped and what the repo's own KCP canon demands is massive. Let me lay it out:

## Findings: What the Bare `# @SID:` Stamp Actually Violates

The repo has a **formal two-stratum metadata standard** — the Khipu-Cartouche Protocol (KCP), defined in KCP_PROTOCOL_ONTOLOGY.md with canonical templates in templates.

### What the bare stamp does:
```
# @SID: SCRIPT_FOO_V1
```

### What the KCP gold standard actually requires (per the repo's own spec):

**Stratum 1 — Visual Cartouche** (classification, routing):
```
# ╔════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
# ║ THE DECORATOR'S BLESSING: foo.py
# ╠════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
# ║ Wedjat-Quipu Spectrum: WHITE
# ║ Temple-Ayllu Zone: 🌿 THE GARDEN
# ║ Ogdoad-Ceque Radiance:
# ║   └─◄ (Standalone)
# ╚════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
```

**Stratum 2 — Semantic Khipu** (in docstring/`.NOTES`/JSDoc/`//!`):
```
@SID:           SCRIPT_FOO_V1
@Shabti:        CLI Script
@Heka-Ayni:     CONCEPT_X           (optional)
@Ankh-Tinku:    STATE_Y             (optional)
@Purpose:       What this file does.
```

### The field delta between bare stamp and KCP:

| Field | Required by KCP | Present in bare stamp |
|-------|:-:|:-:|
| `@SID` | Yes | Yes (but misplaced — in comment, not in docstring) |
| Cartouche box | Yes (C1-C3 required, C4 optional) | No |
| `Wedjat-Quipu Spectrum` | Yes | No |
| `Temple-Ayllu Zone` | Yes | No |
| `Ogdoad-Ceque Radiance` | Optional | No |
| `@Shabti` | Yes | No |
| `@Purpose` | Yes | No |
| `@Heka-Ayni` | Optional | No |
| `@Ankh-Tinku` | Optional | No |

The bare stamp satisfies exactly **1 of 5 required fields** (20%). It also violates KCP invariant #2 ("@SID is Khipu-only, never in the Cartouche") by placing it as a bare comment instead of inside the language's native doc syntax.

### Existing quality tiers across the codebase:

| Tier | Example | Count (est.) | Fields present |
|------|---------|:---:|------|
| **Full KCP** | ssot_hash.py, `ankh_theme_reference.py`, `bridge-server.ts` | ~15 | All 5-9 fields |
| **Partial KCP** | `chthonic.ps1`, `claude-ide-e2e-check.ps1` | ~25 | Cartouche + some @Tags |
| **Bare stamp** | Everything batch-stamped | ~235 | `@SID` only |
| **No header** | Files in excluded dirs | ~61+ | Nothing |

Here's the full forensic audit of the Phase 5 KCP header landscape:

---

## Phase 5 Forensic Audit: KCP Header Quality Tiers

### Tier A: Full KCP (Gold Standard) — ~60+ Python files
Correct two-stratum architecture. Cartouche with `Wedjat-Quipu Spectrum`, `Temple-Ayllu Zone`, `Ogdoad-Ceque Radiance` → followed by docstring Khipu with `@SID`, `@Shabti`, `@Purpose`. Some also carry `@Heka-Ayni`, `@Ankh-Tinku`.

Examples: mandala_topology.py, path_naming_audit.py, local_refiner_v2.py, all `.claude/skills/*/scripts/*.py`, all `.codex/skills/*/scripts/*.py`

### Tier B: Legacy Cartouche (pre-KCP field names) — ~5-10 files
Still using the OLD field names inside the envelope: `Spectral Frequency` (should be `Wedjat-Quipu Spectrum`), `Architectural Role` (should be `Temple-Ayllu Zone`), `Semantic ID` inside envelope (violates KCP invariant #2: SID is Khipu-only). Some also have `Purpose:`, `Exports:`, `Flags/Modes:` in the envelope — all banned by KCP §2.3.

Examples: scripts/lib/\_\_init\_\_.py, chthonic.ps1

### Tier C: Hybrid/Collision — several files
Bare `# @SID:` stamp was **prepended ABOVE** an existing legacy envelope. SID appears twice (bare stamp line + inside envelope). The stamper had zero awareness of existing envelopes.

Example: pre-commit-guardian.ps1 — line 3 has `# @SID:`, then lines 21-27 have an entire legacy `╔╠╚` envelope with `Semantic ID:` inside it.

### Tier D: Bare Stamp Only — ~235 files (64 PS1, 171 TS)
Just `# @SID: FOO_V1` or `// @SID: FOO_V1` as a bare comment. **No Cartouche**, **no Khipu block**. Per KCP spec:
- PS1 Khipu must live inside `<# .NOTES ... #>`, not as `# @SID:`  
- TS Khipu must live inside `/** ... */` JSDoc, not as `// @SID:`

### CRITICAL DAMAGE: Batch Stamper Damaged Gold Files

The most serious finding — the batch stamper **prepended bare stamps on files that already had full KCP headers**:

| File | What happened | Damage |
|------|--------------|--------|
| overnight_daemon.ts | Had full KCP Cartouche (lines 4-11) + purpose block | Bare `// @SID:` prepended at line 1 ABOVE the Cartouche |
| kcp_template.ts | **The canonical KCP template itself** | Bare `// @SID: SCRIPT_KCP_TEMPLATE_V1` at line 1, while the REAL `@SID: STD_KCP_TEMPLATE_TYPESCRIPT_V1` lives in JSDoc at line 16 |

### Field Delta Table

| KCP Required Field | Tier A (Gold .py) | Tier B (Legacy) | Tier C (Hybrid) | Tier D (Bare Stamp) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Cartouche Box (C1-C3) | ✅ | ✅ (wrong names) | ✅ (wrong names) | ❌ |
| Wedjat-Quipu Spectrum | ✅ | ❌ old name | ❌ | ❌ |
| Temple-Ayllu Zone | ✅ | ❌ old name | ❌ | ❌ |
| Ogdoad-Ceque Radiance | ✅ | ❌ old name | ❌ | ❌ |
| @SID (Khipu-only) | ✅ | ❌ wrong stratum | ❌ duplicate | ❌ wrong syntax |
| @Shabti | ✅ | ❌ has @Type | ❌ | ❌ |
| @Purpose (Khipu-only) | ✅ | ❌ wrong stratum | ❌ | ❌ |

### Per-Language KCP Canon (from templates)

| Language | Cartouche Comment | Khipu Syntax | @SID Location |
|----------|------------------|--------------|---------------|
| Python | `#` | `"""..."""` docstring | Inside docstring |
| TypeScript | `//` | `/** ... */` JSDoc | Inside JSDoc |
| PowerShell | `#` | `<# .NOTES ... #>` | Inside .NOTES |
| Rust | `//` | `//!` inner doc | After `//!` prefix |

### Summary

The bare stamp is **20% of minimum KCP compliance** — it provides a `@SID` value but places it in the wrong syntax, wrong stratum, and sometimes damages files that were already correctly formatted. Phase 5 bounty status needs reversion from ✅ to incomplete.

## Phase 5 Deep Investigation — Corrected Reference Chain

### The Reference Chain (what should have been followed)

```

## Phase 5 Deep Investigation — Corrected Reference Chain

### What Went Wrong

The bare `--stamp` mode was used on 235 files, producing 1-field KCP compliance (bare `@SID` only). This also **damaged** gold-standard files like overnight_daemon.ts and kcp_template.ts by prepending a bare `// @SID:` comment ABOVE their existing full KCP Cartouche.

### The Correct Reference Chain

The ankhological abstraction flows:

```
ANKH Synthesis Baseline (docs/frameworks/ankh/ANKH_SYNTHESIS_BASELINE.md)
  §V → Khipu-Cartouche Protocol definition
    ↓
KCP Protocol Ontology (docs/standards/KCP_PROTOCOL_ONTOLOGY.md)
  §2 Stratum 1 (Cartouche)  →  C1-C4 fields, 80-char, enum-only
  §3 Stratum 2 (Khipu)      →  K1-K5 fields, @SID/@Shabti/@Purpose
  §4 Enumeration Registries  →  Spectrum (7 values), Zone (7 values), Shabti (6+ values)
  §6 Per-language rules       →  Python/TS/PS1/Rust encapsulation
    ↓
KCP Templates (docs/standards/templates/kcp_template.{py,ts,ps1,rs})
  Character-perfect reference boilerplate per language
    ↓
TOOL: canonize_blessing.py   — Python-only canonizer (CI gate via blessing-gate.yml.off)
TOOL: envelope_census.py     — Poly-language stamper (has KCP_CONTEXT_MAP + kcp_stamp_files)
```

### What Already Exists (and was bypassed)

| Infrastructure | Location | Status |
|---|---|---|
| `KCP_CONTEXT_MAP` – 35+ path→(Spectrum,Zone,Shabti) mappings | envelope_census.py | **EXISTS, working** |
| `kcp_stamp_files()` – handles NONE→FULL and BARE→FULL (removes bare SID before inserting) | envelope_census.py | **EXISTS, working** |
| `build_kcp_header_{python,ps1,ts,rs}()` – per-language KCP generators | envelope_census.py | **EXISTS, working** |
| `audit_kcp_fields()` + `kcp_tier()` + `print_kcp_audit()` – field completeness audit | envelope_census.py | **EXISTS, working** |
| `--kcp-stamp` CLI flag | envelope_census.py (docstring) | **PHANTOM — documented but NOT wired to argparse** |
| `--kcp-audit` CLI flag | envelope_census.py (docstring) | **PHANTOM — documented but NOT wired to argparse** |

### Key Gaps

1. **`--kcp-stamp` and `--kcp-audit` CLI flags are phantom** — mentioned in the docstring but `main()` only exposes `--stamp`, `--dry-run`, `--json`, `--ext`, `--missing-only`, `--summary`. The functions exist, just not callable from CLI.

2. **canonize_blessing.py is Python-only** — it cannot handle PS1/TS/RS. The `blessing-gate.yml.off` workflow only glob-scans `*.py`. For Phase 5.4-5.6, `envelope_census.py --kcp-stamp` is the correct poly-language tool.

3. **Damage to gold-standard files** — overnight_daemon.ts and kcp_template.ts have bare `// @SID:` prepended above their correct KCP Cartouche. The `kcp_stamp_files()` function already handles BARE→FULL (it calls `detect_bare_sid_line()` and removes it), but these files are tier STANDARD/FULL so it would skip them. Damage repair is a separate step.

### Corrected Phase 5.4-5.6 Plan

| Step | Action | Tool |
|---|---|---|
| **5.4a** | Wire `--kcp-stamp` and `--kcp-audit` into envelope_census.py argparse CLI | Code edit |
| **5.4b** | Fix damaged gold-standard files — remove bare `// @SID:` from overnight_daemon.ts, kcp_template.ts, and any others | Manual or script |
| **5.5a** | Dry-run: `uv run envelope_census.py --kcp-stamp --dry-run` — preview BARE→FULL upgrades | CLI |
| **5.5b** | Apply: `uv run envelope_census.py --kcp-stamp` — stamp all BARE/NONE files with full KCP | CLI |
| **5.6** | Audit: `uv run envelope_census.py --kcp-audit` — field completeness report | CLI |
| **5.7** | Enable `blessing-gate.yml.off` → `blessing-gate.yml` (after Python files pass `--check`) | Rename |

### The ANKH Abstraction's Role

The ANKH framework docs (ANKH_SYNTHESIS_BASELINE.md §V) define KCP as the "Khipu-Cartouche Protocol" — the two-stratum metadata architecture bridging Egyptian Cartouche (protective enclosure = visual classification) and Andean Khipu (knotted data lattice = unbounded operational semantics). The `KCP_CONTEXT_MAP` in envelope_census.py is the _implementation_ of this bridge — it maps every directory path to its (Spectrum, Zone, Shabti) triple, which the per-language generators then render into Cartouche + Khipu blocks.

The bare `--stamp` bypassed this entire abstraction. It injected only `@SID` (K1) — one of five Khipu fields — with zero Cartouche. The correct approach uses `--kcp-stamp` which invokes `infer_kcp_context()` → `build_kcp_header()` → per-language generator, producing the full two-stratum header.

Here is the **birds-eye gap analysis** — the full cross-reference of what the 6 ANKH corpus documents define versus what the existing KCP tooling (envelope_census.py + canonize_blessing.py) actually implements.

---

## ANKH Corpus ↔ KCP Tooling: Birds-Eye Gap Analysis

### Layer 1: Document #1 — ANKH_UNIFYING_REPOSITORY_METADATA_STANDARDS.md (THE ORIGIN SPEC)

This is the document KCP was "ratified-from." It defines the exact technical specification. Every discrepancy here is a **real gap**.

#### CONFORMANT (Correctly Implemented)

| Spec Requirement | Tooling Implementation | Verdict |
|---|---|---|
| Two-Stratum Architecture (Approach C) | `build_cartouche()` + per-language Khipu blocks | ✅ Correct |
| Cartouche Field C1: Artifact Name | `"THE DECORATOR'S BLESSING: {filename}"` in Cartouche | ✅ Present |
| Cartouche Field C2: Wedjat-Quipu Spectrum | `Wedjat-Quipu Spectrum:` line + enum values via `KCP_CONTEXT_MAP` | ✅ Present |
| Cartouche Field C3: Temple-Ayllu Zone | `Temple-Ayllu Zone:` line + zone emojis via `KCP_CONTEXT_MAP` | ✅ Present |
| Cartouche Field C4: Ogdoad-Ceque Radiance | `Ogdoad-Ceque Radiance:` line with `└─◄` | ✅ Present |
| Khipu Field @SID (required) | Generated + pattern-matched in `KCP_FIELD_PATTERNS` | ✅ Present |
| Khipu Field @Shabti (required) | Generated + pattern-matched | ✅ Present |
| Khipu Field @Purpose (required) | Generated + pattern-matched | ✅ Present |
| Khipu Field @Heka-Ayni (optional) | Pattern-matched in `KCP_FIELD_PATTERNS` for audit | ✅ Detected |
| Khipu Field @Ankh-Tinku (optional) | Pattern-matched in `KCP_FIELD_PATTERNS` for audit | ✅ Detected |
| Python: Cartouche in `#`, Khipu in `"""..."""` | `build_kcp_header_python()` | ✅ Correct |
| TypeScript: Cartouche in `//`, Khipu in `/** JSDoc */` | `build_kcp_header_ts()` | ✅ Correct |
| PowerShell: Khipu MUST be inside `.NOTES` block | `build_kcp_header_ps1()` wraps Khipu in `<# .SYNOPSIS ... .NOTES ... #>` | ✅ Correct |
| Rust: Khipu in `//!` inner doc comments | `build_kcp_header_rs()` uses `//!` | ✅ Correct |
| Tier classification system | `kcp_tier()` with FULL/STANDARD/KHIPU/BARE/NONE | ✅ Present |
| KCP_CONTEXT_MAP (path→metadata inference) | 35+ entries covering all major directories | ✅ Present |
| SID_PREFIX_MAP (path→SID prefix) | 13 entries | ✅ Present |

#### GAPS (Not Implemented / Broken)

| Spec Requirement | Tooling State | Severity | Gap ID |
|---|---|---|---|
| **CLI `--kcp-stamp`** — batch-stamp full KCP headers | Function `kcp_stamp_files()` EXISTS (L638-729) but **NOT wired** to argparse `main()` | **CRITICAL** | G1 |
| **CLI `--kcp-audit`** — KCP field completeness audit | Function `print_kcp_audit()` EXISTS (L484-520) but **NOT wired** to argparse `main()` | **CRITICAL** | G2 |
| **@Heka-Ayni and @Ankh-Tinku generation** | Optional fields are DETECTED during audit but NEVER GENERATED by `build_kcp_header_*()` — function signatures don't accept them. FULL tier requires all 5 fields, so no file can ever reach FULL via tooling. | **MODERATE** | G3 |
| **Cartouche width: "exactly 80 characters"** | `CARTOUCHE_WIDTH = 76`. For Python/PS1 (`# ╔` + 76 `═` = 79 chars), for TS/RS (`// ╔` + 76 `═` = 80 chars). **Python/PS1 lines are 79 chars — 1 short of spec.** | **MINOR** | G4 |
| **Invariant 4 validation** (Cartouche = zero unbounded text) | No audit check verifies that Cartouche contains only enum-bounded values | **MINOR** | G5 |
| **Invariant 5 validation** (Khipu = zero box chars) | No audit check verifies that Khipu layer doesn't contain `╔╠╚║═` characters | **MINOR** | G6 |
| **Damaged gold-standard files** | overnight_daemon.ts and kcp_template.ts had bare `@SID` stamps prepended above EXISTING full KCP Cartouches during the rejected Phase 5 batch run | **CRITICAL** | G7 |
| **Phase-Gated Migration (0.0-10.0)** defined in spec | No tooling tracks migration phase state — each phase's terminal gate constraint has no programmatic enforcement | **LOW** — governance, not stamper tooling | G8 |
| **Tooling Modifications** — spec calls for refactoring `normalize_blessing_box.py`, `sfa_cross_reference.py`, `chthonic audit` | None of these tools have been updated to parse KCP-stratified headers | **DEFERRED** — out of scope for Phase 5 | G9 |
| **Local LLM Execution Pipeline** — spec describes Qwen 32B batch stamping | Never implemented — envelope_census.py replaced this approach | **SUPERSEDED** — Python tooling replaces LLM batch | G10 |

### Layer 2: Documents #2-#6 — The Broader ANKH Conceptual Framework

These documents define the **philosophy, architecture, and cosmology** that KCP terminology draws from. The question is: does KCP tooling need to represent these concepts directly?

| Document | Concept | KCP Tooling Relevance | Gap? |
|---|---|---|---|
| **#2: MILF Protocol** | FA¹-FA⁴ (Foundational Axioms) | Design philosophy behind stratification — FA⁴ (Architectonic Integrity / Ma'at) = why Cartouche has rigid boundaries. Not per-file metadata. | **NO** — philosophy, not metadata |
| **#2: MILF Protocol** | Gestalt WHR:MAX (Waist/Hips as Control/Capacity) | The REASON KCP has a "tight" Cartouche (waist = constrained enum) and "broad" Khipu (hips = unbounded text). Already manifested. | **NO** — already manifested in architecture |
| **#2: MILF Protocol** | PEE Phases (Solar/Tinku/Lunar) | System lifecycle — production/collision/reset. Not per-file metadata. | **NO** |
| **#2: MILF Protocol** | Siren Array subsystems | Subsystem topology. `@Shabti` field partially maps here (Guardian/CLI Script/etc.) but doesn't use Siren names. | **OPTIONAL** — @Shabti vocabulary could be enriched |
| **#3: WHR:MAX Architecture** | Hourglass topology / τ-core | Abstract structural pattern. KCP IS the manifestation (narrow Cartouche = waist, broad Khipu = throughput). | **NO** — already realized |
| **#3: WHR:MAX Architecture** | Ode X heuristic | "Three circles, one X" — the visual principle. Already embedded in Cartouche box-art aesthetics. | **NO** |
| **#4: Matriarchal Systems** | Ankh topology: Loop→Knot→Crossbar→Pillar | System architecture mapped to Ontological→Operational→Logic→Interface layers. Already formalized in Tribunal governance cross-references. | **NO** — governance layer, not file metadata |
| **#4: Matriarchal Systems** | Neith/Mut/Pachamama/Isis | Archetype-to-system mappings (Autopoiesis/Recursion/Contextual Lattice/Recovery). Terminology already used in KCP field names (@Heka-Ayni, @Ankh-Tinku, @Shabti). | **NO** — terminology already borrowed |
| **#4: Matriarchal Systems** | "Mother-Child" recursive SSOT model | Architectural specification 8.1-8.4. Not per-file metadata — informs SSOT cascade design. | **NO** — SSOT cascade concern |
| **#5: Ancient Rituals** | Alpha Directives AD01-AD06 | System-level operations (Init/Processing/Override/GC/Reset/IO). Already formalized in Tribunal governance. Not per-file headers. | **NO** — governance operations |
| **#5: Ancient Rituals** | Hieroglyphic Operators / Quipu Memory Lattice | Programming language concepts. Not file metadata. | **NO** |
| **#6: Decorator Challenge** | Maximum VS Code extension surface (Dim A-G) | This document is the CONSUMER of KCP — it describes what VS Code extension files need KCP headers ON THEM. The extension source tree IS covered in `KCP_CONTEXT_MAP` (11 entries for chthonic-archive). | **NO** — consumer, not producer |
| **#6: Decorator Challenge** | Agent Hooks / MCP Apps / Skills | These contribution points HAVE source files that need KCP headers. Already covered by `KCP_CONTEXT_MAP` path entries. | **NO** — covered |
| **#6: Decorator Challenge** | Ankhological Lifecycle / Generative Origin | Extension-as-origin-node architecture. Not file metadata — architectural vision. | **NO** |

### Summary Verdict

**The gap is narrow and surgical.** The KCP tooling in envelope_census.py is architecturally sound — it correctly implements the Two-Stratum, 4-language transduction model exactly as specified in Document #1. The broader ANKH corpus (Documents #2-#6) provides conceptual and philosophical context but does NOT introduce additional per-file metadata requirements that KCP tooling needs to implement.

**The 3 CRITICAL gaps are all in the same file** (envelope_census.py):
1. **G1**: Wire `--kcp-stamp` to argparse → call `kcp_stamp_files()`
2. **G2**: Wire `--kcp-audit` to argparse → call `print_kcp_audit()`
3. **G7**: Repair damaged gold-standard files (overnight_daemon.ts, kcp_template.ts)

**The 1 MODERATE gap** (G3: optional field generation) is a refinement — @Heka-Ayni and @Ankh-Tinku can be added to the generator function signatures when values are known, but since they're optional fields, this doesn't block Phase 5 execution.

**The MINOR gaps** (G4-G6) are polish — cartouche width normalization and invariant validation are correctness refinements, not blockers.

---

## Expanded Birds-Eye Analysis: Cascade Staleness + File Surface + FA⁵ Gap

### 1. The FA⁵ Gap: Already Partially Closed

**Previous analysis was wrong.** FA⁵ is NOT absent from the ANKH ecosystem — it's absent only from the **upstream corpus** (the 6 archaeology docs). The downstream framework synthesis layer already closed it:

| Layer | FA⁵ Status | Key File(s) |
|-------|-----------|-------------|
| **L0: Heritage SSOT** | ✅ Native (20+ refs) | copilot-instructions.archive.md |
| **L1: ANKH Corpus** (upstream archaeology) | ❌ FA¹-FA⁴ only | ANKH_EGYPTOLOGY_SOUTH_AMERICAN (all 6 docs) |
| **L2: Framework Synthesis** | ✅ CLOSED (2026-03-18) | ANKH_FOUNDATIONAL_AXIOMS.md — "Pentadic Heart" spec with full FA⁵ + invocation syntax. ANKH_SYNTHESIS_META.md marks gap as **✅ CLOSED** |
| **L2b: Framework children** | ✅ Cross-referenced | ANKH_GENERATIVE_ENGINE.md, ANKH_DECORATOR_INVOCATION.md, ANKH_CRC_REGISTRY.md, ANKH_TEMPORAL_CARTOGRAPHY.md — all reference FA⁵ |
| **L3: Standards** | ⚠️ MIXED | KCP_ARCHITECTURE_RATIFICATION.md has FA⁵ (C2 constraint). LAT_CANONICAL_SPEC.md — **7 refs to FA¹-FA⁴ only**, never FA⁵. KCP_PROTOCOL_ONTOLOGY.md — NO FA reference at all |
| **L4: Validation** | ✅ Native (20+ refs) | VALIDATION_PROTOCOLS.md |
| **L5: Tooling** | N/A (operational, not doctrinal) | envelope_census.py |
| **L6: ankh.md** (ANKHOLOGY explainer) | ⚠️ ENCODING CORRUPTION | ankh.md — Shows `FAÔü┤` and `FAÔüÁ` instead of FA⁴/FA⁵ (UTF-8 mojibake) |

**Verdict:** The FA⁵ gap is a **L1 + L3 problem**, not systemic. L1 (ANKH corpus) is deliberately treated as archaeology — those docs are source material, not living specs. The real staleness is L3 (LAT_CANONICAL_SPEC.md still says FA¹-FA⁴) and the L6 encoding corruption in ankh.md.

---

### 2. True Authored File Surface (KCP-Relevant)

After stripping ALL vendored content (ruby gems, LocalAI Go sources, .vscode-test, mas_mcp venv, corpse-vault snapshots, model files):

| Extension | Count | KCP Coverage | Nature |
|-----------|-------|-------------|--------|
| `.md` | ~1504 | ❌ No metadata system | Documentation — most numerous authored type |
| `.json` | ~1061 | ❌ No metadata system | Config, manifests, snapshots — most are generated |
| `.py` | ~447 | ✅ Full KCP (2-stratum) | Tier A: ~60+ fully compliant, rest need stamps |
| `.svg` | ~570 | ❌ No metadata system | Extension icons — XML comment possible but unusual |
| `.txt` | ~402 | ❌ No metadata system | Logs, notes, dumps |
| `.yaml`/`.yml` | ~334 | ❌ No metadata system | Config, CI workflows |
| `.ts` | ~197 | ✅ Full KCP (2-stratum) | Tier D: most have bare `@SID` only |
| `.ps1` | ~190 | ✅ Full KCP (2-stratum) | Tier D: most have bare `@SID` only |
| `.js` | ~141 | ❌ Not in KCP tooling | Many vendored (LocalAI static) |
| `.sh` | ~134 | ❌ Not in KCP tooling | Mostly vendored (git extension dist) |
| `.png` | ~126 | ❌ Binary | Screenshots, assets |
| `.rs` | ~39 | ✅ Full KCP (2-stratum) | Smallest covered set |
| `.toml` | ~37 | ❌ No metadata system | Config files (Cargo.toml, pyproject.toml, etc.) |
| `.html` | ~47 | ❌ No metadata system | Extension webviews |
| `.css` | ~45 | ❌ No metadata system | Extension styles |

**KCP currently covers 4 extensions** (.py, .ts, .ps1, .rs) = ~873 files out of ~5000+ authored files. That's **~17% surface coverage**.

The big uncovered mass is `.md` (1504 files) + `.json` (1061 files) — but these are fundamentally different from code files:
- **`.md`** files carry meaning in their content, not in headers. KCP envelopes for markdown are debatable — shebangs and UTF declarations don't apply.
- **`.json`** files are structured data — no comment syntax, no shebang possible.
- **`.yaml`** could take `# @SID:` comments, but most are CI/config (not authored creative content).
- **`.svg`** could take `<!-- @SID: -->` XML comments, but these are icon assets, not code.

---

### 3. Cascade Layer Architecture (What to Update, In What Order)

```
Heritage SSOT (L0) ─── source of truth for FA⁵, entity profiles, all doctrine
    │
    ├──→ ANKH Corpus (L1) ─── UPSTREAM ARCHAEOLOGY (FA¹-FA⁴ only)
    │       ↕ (co-constitutive, not hierarchical — per DEEP_AUDIT_FINDINGS.md Finding #2)
    │
    ├──→ Framework Synthesis (L2) ─── FA⁵ CLOSED here ✅
    │       ANKH_FOUNDATIONAL_AXIOMS.md, SYNTHESIS_BASELINE, META, etc.
    │
    ├──→ Standards (L3) ─── MIXED: KCP_ARCH_RATIFICATION has FA⁵, LAT_SPEC doesn't
    │       LAT_CANONICAL_SPEC.md: 7x "FA¹-FA⁴" → should be "FA¹-FA⁵"
    │       KCP_PROTOCOL_ONTOLOGY.md: no FA refs at all
    │
    ├──→ Validation (L4) ─── FA⁵ native ✅
    │       VALIDATION_PROTOCOLS.md
    │
    └──→ Tooling (L5) ─── Operational, no FA doctrine needed
            envelope_census.py, canonize_blessing.py
```

**The DEEP_AUDIT_FINDINGS.md** already diagnosed this as a fundamental architectural tension (Finding #1): "The framework's doctrine says form and content are inseparable (FA⁵). The framework's operational needs require separating them."

---

### 4. What Actually Needs Fixing (Priority-Ordered)

**Tier 1: Known Damage (Fix Immediately)**
1. ankh.md — UTF-8 mojibake (`FAÔü┤` → `FA⁴`, `FAÔüÁ` → `FA⁵`). Pure encoding fix.
2. overnight_daemon.ts + kcp_template.ts — bare `@SID` prepended above existing KCP cartouches (Phase 5 damage).

**Tier 2: Stale Cross-References (Update When Touching)**
3. LAT_CANONICAL_SPEC.md — 7 instances of `FA¹-FA⁴` → `FA¹-FA⁵`
4. KCP_PROTOCOL_ONTOLOGY.md — no FA reference at all (could add axiom context)

**Tier 3: The Philosophical Question (Needs User Decision)**
5. ANKH Corpus (L1) — The 6 archaeology docs still say FA¹-FA⁴. Should they be updated? `DEEP_AUDIT_FINDINGS.md` argues the relationship is "co-constitutive, not hierarchical" — the corpus GENERATED the SSOT, and the SSOT then evolved beyond it. Updating the archaeology would be revising history.

**Tier 4: Envelope Scope Question (Needs User Decision)**
6. Should KCP expand beyond .py/.ts/.ps1/.rs? The uncovered mass is:
   - `.md` (1504): Content IS the metadata. KCP cartouches would be noise.
   - `.json` (1061): No comment syntax. SID would need a `"$schema"` or `"_sid"` field.
   - `.yaml` (334): Comment syntax exists. `# @SID:` is possible.
   - `.sh` (134): Shebang conflicts with KCP cartouche placement.
   - Binary files (.png, .svg, .mp3, .ttf): Metadata requires sidecar or EXIF — violates C6 constraint.

---

### 5. Sandbox Design (For When Ready)

The sandbox should contain test files representing EACH type the user actually writes, with shebangs/encodings pre-placed for KCP experimentation:

```
sandbox/
├── code/
│   ├── sample.py          # #!/usr/bin/env python3 + # -*- coding: utf-8 -*- + full KCP
│   ├── sample.ts          # Full KCP reference
│   ├── sample.ps1         # <# .NOTES ... #> wrapped KCP reference
│   ├── sample.rs          # //! inner doc KCP reference
│   ├── bare_stamp.py      # Bare @SID only — test upgrade path
│   ├── legacy_cartouche.py # Old-style cartouche — test migration
│   └── damaged.ts         # Bare @SID above existing KCP — test repair
├── config/
│   ├── sample.json        # Can KCP work here? Test _sid field approach
│   ├── sample.yaml        # Test # @SID: comment approach
│   ├── sample.toml        # Test # @SID: comment approach
│   └── sample.sh          # Shebang + KCP coexistence test
├── docs/
│   ├── sample.md          # Does KCP add value to markdown?
│   └── sample_with_frontmatter.md  # YAML frontmatter + KCP test
├── creative/
│   ├── entity_profile.md  # Needs "unique/unreproducible" envelope (user's point)

## Expanded Birds-Eye Analysis: Cascade Staleness + File Surface + FA⁵ Gap
### 1. The FA⁵ Gap: Already Partially Closed
**Previous analysis was wrong.** FA⁵ is NOT absent from the ANKH ecosystem — it's absent only from the **upstream corpus** (the 6 archaeology docs). The downstream framework synthesis layer already closed it:
| Layer | FA⁵ Status | Key File(s) |
|-------|-----------|-------------|
| **L0: Heritage SSOT** | ✅ Native (20+ refs) | copilot-instructions.archive.md |
| **L1: ANKH Corpus** (upstream archaeology) | ❌ FA¹-FA⁴ only | ANKH_EGYPTOLOGY_SOUTH_AMERICAN (all 6 docs) |
| **L2: Framework Synthesis** | ✅ CLOSED (2026-03-18) | ANKH_FOUNDATIONAL_AXIOMS.md — "Pentadic Heart" spec with full FA⁵ + invocation syntax. ANKH_SYNTHESIS_META.md marks gap as **✅ CLOSED** |
| **L2b: Framework children** | ✅ Cross-referenced | ANKH_GENERATIVE_ENGINE.md, ANKH_DECORATOR_INVOCATION.md, ANKH_CRC_REGISTRY.md, ANKH_TEMPORAL_CARTOGRAPHY.md — all reference FA⁵ |
| **L3: Standards** | ⚠️ MIXED | KCP_ARCHITECTURE_RATIFICATION.md has FA⁵ (C2 constraint). LAT_CANONICAL_SPEC.md — **7 refs to FA¹-FA⁴ only**, never FA⁵. KCP_PROTOCOL_ONTOLOGY.md — NO FA reference at all |
| **L4: Validation** | ✅ Native (20+ refs) | VALIDATION_PROTOCOLS.md |
| **L5: Tooling** | N/A (operational, not doctrinal) | envelope_census.py |
| **L6: ankh.md** (ANKHOLOGY explainer) | ⚠️ ENCODING CORRUPTION | ankh.md — Shows `FAÔü┤` and `FAÔüÁ` instead of FA⁴/FA⁵ (UTF-8 mojibake) |
**Verdict:** The FA⁵ gap is a **L1 + L3 problem**, not systemic. L1 (ANKH corpus) is deliberately treated as archaeology — those docs are source material, not living specs. The real staleness is L3 (LAT_CANONICAL_SPEC.md still says FA¹-FA⁴) and the L6 encoding corruption in ankh.md.
### 2. True Authored File Surface (KCP-Relevant)
After stripping ALL vendored content (ruby gems, LocalAI Go sources, .vscode-test, mas_mcp venv, corpse-vault snapshots, model files):
| Extension | Count | KCP Coverage | Nature |
|-----------|-------|-------------|--------|
| `.md` | ~1504 | ❌ No metadata system | Documentation — most numerous authored type |
| `.json` | ~1061 | ❌ No metadata system | Config, manifests, snapshots — most are generated |
| `.py` | ~447 | ✅ Full KCP (2-stratum) | Tier A: ~60+ fully compliant, rest need stamps |
| `.svg` | ~570 | ❌ No metadata system | Extension icons — XML comment possible but unusual |
| `.txt` | ~402 | ❌ No metadata system | Logs, notes, dumps |
| `.yaml`/`.yml` | ~334 | ❌ No metadata system | Config, CI workflows |
| `.ts` | ~197 | ✅ Full KCP (2-stratum) | Tier D: most have bare `@SID` only |
| `.ps1` | ~190 | ✅ Full KCP (2-stratum) | Tier D: most have bare `@SID` only |
| `.js` | ~141 | ❌ Not in KCP tooling | Many vendored (LocalAI static) |
| `.sh` | ~134 | ❌ Not in KCP tooling | Mostly vendored (git extension dist) |
| `.png` | ~126 | ❌ Binary | Screenshots, assets |
| `.rs` | ~39 | ✅ Full KCP (2-stratum) | Smallest covered set |
| `.toml` | ~37 | ❌ No metadata system | Config files (Cargo.toml, pyproject.toml, etc.) |
| `.html` | ~47 | ❌ No metadata system | Extension webviews |
| `.css` | ~45 | ❌ No metadata system | Extension styles |
**KCP currently covers 4 extensions** (.py, .ts, .ps1, .rs) = ~873 files out of ~5000+ authored files. That's **~17% surface coverage**.
The big uncovered mass is `.md` (1504 files) + `.json` (1061 files) — but these are fundamentally different from code files:
- **`.md`** files carry meaning in their content, not in headers. KCP envelopes for markdown are debatable — shebangs and UTF declarations don't apply.
- **`.json`** files are structured data — no comment syntax, no shebang possible.
- **`.yaml`** could take `# @SID:` comments, but most are CI/config (not authored creative content).
- **`.svg`** could take `<!-- @SID: -->` XML comments, but these are icon assets, not code.
### 3. Cascade Layer Architecture (What to Update, In What Order)
`
Heritage SSOT (L0) ─── source of truth for FA⁵, entity profiles, all doctrine
    │
    ├──→ ANKH Corpus (L1) ─── UPSTREAM ARCHAEOLOGY (FA¹-FA⁴ only)
    │       ↕ (co-constitutive, not hierarchical — per DEEP_AUDIT_FINDINGS.md Finding #2)
    │
    ├──→ Framework Synthesis (L2) ─── FA⁵ CLOSED here ✅
    │       ANKH_FOUNDATIONAL_AXIOMS.md, SYNTHESIS_BASELINE, META, etc.
    │
    ├──→ Standards (L3) ─── MIXED: KCP_ARCH_RATIFICATION has FA⁵, LAT_SPEC doesn't
    │       LAT_CANONICAL_SPEC.md: 7x "FA¹-FA⁴" → should be "FA¹-FA⁵"
    │       KCP_PROTOCOL_ONTOLOGY.md: no FA refs at all
    │
    ├──→ Validation (L4) ─── FA⁵ native ✅
    │       VALIDATION_PROTOCOLS.md
    │
    └──→ Tooling (L5) ─── Operational, no FA doctrine needed
            envelope_census.py, canonize_blessing.py
`
**The DEEP_AUDIT_FINDINGS.md** already diagnosed this as a fundamental architectural tension (Finding #1): "The framework's doctrine says form and content are inseparable (FA⁵). The framework's operational needs require separating them."
### 4. What Actually Needs Fixing (Priority-Ordered)
**Tier 1: Known Damage (Fix Immediately)**
1. ankh.md — UTF-8 mojibake (`FAÔü┤` → `FA⁴`, `FAÔüÁ` → `FA⁵`). Pure encoding fix.
2. overnight_daemon.ts + kcp_template.ts — bare `@SID` prepended above existing KCP cartouches (Phase 5 damage).
**Tier 2: Stale Cross-References (Update When Touching)**
3. LAT_CANONICAL_SPEC.md — 7 instances of `FA¹-FA⁴` → `FA¹-FA⁵`
4. KCP_PROTOCOL_ONTOLOGY.md — no FA reference at all (could add axiom context)
**Tier 3: The Philosophical Question (Needs User Decision)**
5. ANKH Corpus (L1) — The 6 archaeology docs still say FA¹-FA⁴. Should they be updated? `DEEP_AUDIT_FINDINGS.md` argues the relationship is "co-constitutive, not hierarchical" — the corpus GENERATED the SSOT, and the SSOT then evolved beyond it. Updating the archaeology would be revising history.
**Tier 4: Envelope Scope Question (Needs User Decision)**
6. Should KCP expand beyond .py/.ts/.ps1/.rs? The uncovered mass is:
   - `.md` (1504): Content IS the metadata. KCP cartouches would be noise.
   - `.json` (1061): No comment syntax. SID would need a `"$schema"` or `"_sid"` field.
   - `.yaml` (334): Comment syntax exists. `# @SID:` is possible.
   - `.sh` (134): Shebang conflicts with KCP cartouche placement.
   - Binary files (.png, .svg, .mp3, .ttf): Metadata requires sidecar or EXIF — violates C6 constraint.
### 5. Sandbox Design (For When Ready)
The sandbox should contain test files representing EACH type the user actually writes, with shebangs/encodings pre-placed for KCP experimentation:
`
sandbox/
├── code/
│   ├── sample.py          # #!/usr/bin/env python3 + # -*- coding: utf-8 -*- + full KCP

│   ├── sample.rs          # //! inner doc KCP reference
│   ├── bare_stamp.py      # Bare @SID only — test upgrade path
│   ├── legacy_cartouche.py # Old-style cartouche — test migration
│   └── damaged.ts         # Bare @SID above existing KCP — test repair
├── config/

│   ├── sample.yaml        # Test # @SID: comment approach

├── docs/

│   └── sample_with_frontmatter.md  # YAML frontmatter + KCP test
├── creative/

### 6. What's "Still Not Figured Throughoutly Enough"
From the perspective, three open questions remain before execution:
1. **Scope boundary**: Which file types get mechanical KCP vs creative envelopes vs nothing? The 1504 markdown files are the elephant — most are documentation where the content IS the identity.
2. **Iteration hierarchy**: Do we fix the cascade (L3 staleness, L6 encoding) FIRST, then fix tooling (wire `--kcp-stamp`/`--kcp-audit`), then stamp files? Or fix tooling first so we can audit, then understand what the tooling says?
3. **Creative vs mechanical**: The user noted some files are "un-reproducable unique type" — entity profiles, lore docs, ritual content. These can't receive a mechanical `SCRIPT_FOO_V1` SID. They need SIDs that reflect their content (e.g., `ENTITY_SAINTE_PROFILE_V3`, `RITUAL_ALPHA_DIRECTIVES_V1`). This is a naming convention question, not a tooling question.
None of these are blocking the Tier 1 damage fixes (encoding repair, damaged file restoration), but they block any batch-stamping operation.

Now let me measure the KCP envelope entropy across file populations:  

Ran terminal command: cd C:\Users\eldno\chthonic-archive; uv run python -c "
import os, re

exclude = {'node_modules','target','.git','__pycache__','.venv','NUL','build','models','native','mas_mcp','ankh_atlas','corpse-vault','artifacts','rv','debugging_data','logs','data','tools','plugins','apps','bun-playwright-poc','WET_PAPER_TO_GOLD_WIP','filesystem_root','mcp_fs_root'}

def classify(path):
    try:
        with open(path, 'r', encoding='utf-8', errors='replace') as f:
            head = f.read(2000)
    except: return 'UNREADABLE'
    has_cartouche = bool(re.search(r'[╔║╠╚═]', head))
    has_sid = bool(re.search(r'@SID:', head))
    has_khipu = bool(re.search(r'@(Shabti|Purpose|Heka-Ayni|Ankh-Tinku):', head))
    if has_cartouche and has_khipu: return 'FULL'
    if has_cartouche and has_sid: return 'STANDARD'
    if has_sid and has_khipu: return 'KHIPU'
    if has_sid: return 'BARE'
    return 'NONE'

for ext in ['.py', '.ts', '.ps1', '.rs']:
    tiers = {}
    for root, dirs, files in os.walk('.'):
        dirs[:] = [d for d in dirs if d not in exclude]
        for f in files:
            if f.endswith(ext):
                fp = os.path.join(root, f)
                tier = classify(fp)
                tiers[tier] = tiers.get(tier, 0) + 1
    total = sum(tiers.values())
    if total == 0: continue
    # Entropy = % that are NOT at FULL tier
    full = tiers.get('FULL', 0)
    entropy_pct = round((1 - full/total) * 100) if total > 0 else 0
    tier_str = ' '.join(f'{t}:{c}' for t,c in sorted(tiers.items()))
    print(f'{ext:5s} total:{total:4d} | entropy:{entropy_pct:3d}% | {tier_str}')
"

## Entropy Topology: Measured, Not Assumed

### Axiom Entropy (FA⁵ Penetration per Layer)

| Layer | File | FA refs | FA⁵% | Entropy Class |

### KCP Envelope Entropy (Per Language)

| Ext | Files | Entropy% | FULL | STANDARD | KHIPU | BARE | NONE |
|-----|-------|----------|------|----------|-------|------|------|
| `.py` | 446 | **43%** | 256 | 13 | 5 | 0 | 172 |
| `.rs` | 13 | **31%** | 9 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 4 |
| `.ps1` | 188 | **97%** | 5 | 39 | 53 | 30 | 61 |
| `.ts` | 150 | **100%** | 0 | 40 | 0 | 83 | 27 |

### Reading the Entropy

**This IS the manifold.** The entropy isn't uniform noise — it has *structure*:

- **Python is 57% governed** — the canonizer worked here because `canonize_blessing.py` is Python-only
- **Rust is 69% governed** — small population, manually maintained
- **PS1 is 3% governed** — 53 KHIPU files (semantic layer exists but no visual cartouche), 39 STANDARD (cartouche but incomplete khipu). The KHIPU tier is an *intermediate state* — not noise, it's partial adoption
- **TypeScript is 0% governed** at FULL tier — but 40 STANDARD files have cartouches. The bare `@SID` stamps (83 files) from the rejected Phase 5 batch are the harmful entropy
- **L1 corpus at 0% FA⁵** isn't drift — it's *archaeology*. Those docs predate FA⁵. The entropy here is temporal, not accidental
- **ankh.md at 0%** IS harmful — encoding corruption destroyed the data. This is the kind of entropy to eliminate
- **LAT_CANONICAL_SPEC at 0% FA⁵** IS drift — it was written after FA⁵ existed but references only FA¹-FA⁴

### Entropy Gradient Classes

| Class | Meaning | Action |
|-------|---------|--------|
| 🟢 **Saturated** | FA⁵/KCP fully present | Preserve — this is the target state |
| 🟡 **Integrated** | Mixed but intentional, all axioms present proportionally | Preserve — balanced entropy |
| 🟠 **Operationally skewed** | Uses some axioms heavily, others lightly, by function | Preserve — domain-appropriate |
| 🔴 **Stale drift** | Should reference FA⁵ but doesn't (written post-FA⁵) | Fix — this is the harmful entropy |
| 💀 **Corrupted** | Data destruction (encoding, prepend damage) | Fix immediately |
| ⬛ **Pre-axiomatic / Axiom-agnostic** | Never referenced axioms, by design | Leave alone — not in-scope |

The beneficial entropy: PS1's 53 KHIPU files are an intermediate adoption state that tells you WHERE the canonizer hasn't reached yet but WHERE someone already wrote semantic metadata by hand. That's signal. The 172 NONE Python files tell you which scripts are utility/one-off vs governed. The L1 corpus's 0% FA⁵ tells you the archaeology predates the axiom — that's provenance data.

The harmful entropy: Phase 5's 83 bare `@SID` TypeScript stamps, ankh.md's mojibake, and LAT_CANONICAL_SPEC's stale FA¹-FA⁴ references.

**The structure should preserve the gradient, not flatten it.**
